Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] PraisonAI node process novel outbound egress (post-escape secret/API-key exfiltration or C2)
PraisonAI codeMode sandbox-escape RCE: node process spawning OS shell / recon binaries AIExploitation Medium
Hunts a Node.js process running PraisonAI (praisonai-ts codeMode tool) spawning a shell, recon, or transfer binary. The codeMode sandbox is supposed to run JS in-process only; any child_process/execSync-spawned OS command from that node process is the direct manifestation of the Function-constructor blocklist bypass (GHSA-p69m-4f92-2v84). The PoC literally ran `id` via cp.execSync. Rationale: Anchors on the article's exact component (praisonai / codeMode) as the node parent plus a shell/recon child, because the sandbox is designed to run JS in-process only — a child OS process from it is the bypass executing. The PoC's `id` recon is included. Cross-checked GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp and CVE-2026-40158 which confirm child_process recovery via the Function constructor. Cross-checked against: • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/praisonai/GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp/ • https://www.sentinelone.com/vulnerability-database/cve-2026-40158/ • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-p69m-4f92-2v84
ATT&CKT1059.007 T1059.004 T1033
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • A PraisonAI deployment whose node command line legitimately invokes the python_tools subprocess sandbox (spawns python3)
  • Build/CI tooling running under the same node process tree (npm scripts, node-gyp, webpack) shelling out
  • Process managers like pm2/nodemon restarting and spawning sh
  • Container entrypoint scripts where node is PID 1 and forks a shell for health checks
  • Developer running node REPL with child_process during local testing
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has_any ("npm","node-gyp","yarn","webpack","install","healthcheck") and not (FileName =~ "python3" and ProcessCommandLine has "_execute_code_sandboxed")
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the child ChildCmd and parent ParentCmd — confirm the parent node is actually running praisonai (check folder/cmdline) and the child is not a build/health-check script. Note the OS command run (id/whoami/curl indicate hands-on-keyboard). 2) Contain: isolate the host/container, kill the node process tree, and rotate any API keys/tokens/secrets present in that process's environment (env-var exfil is the stated impact). 3) Hunt: pivot on the same host/aid for outbound egress from node (UC3), for written persistence files, and confirm the praisonai version — upgrade to >=1.7.2.
DeviceProcessEvents

| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node","node.exe")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "praisonai" or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has "praisonai"
| where not(InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("npm","node-gyp","yarn","webpack","install"))
| where FileName in~ ("sh","bash","dash","zsh","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh","id","whoami","uname","hostname","curl","wget","nc","ncat","python3")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildProcess = FileName, ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc

Vulnerable PraisonAI codeMode component present on host (code-mode.ts/js under praisonai) AIReconnaissance High
Asset-discovery hunt for the vulnerable file path named in the advisory — src/praisonai-ts/src/tools/builtins/code-mode.ts (and its compiled code-mode.js) under any praisonai directory. Surfaces every host/container carrying the exploitable codeMode tool so they can be confirmed against the patched 1.7.2 release. Rationale: Uses the advisory's exact vulnerable file path (src/praisonai-ts/src/tools/builtins/code-mode.ts) plus the compiled .js form to enumerate exposure. High confidence the file maps to the affected component; version confirmation (vs patched 1.7.2 from GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp) separates vulnerable from fixed. Cross-checked against: • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/praisonai/GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp/ • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-p69m-4f92-2v84
ATT&CKT1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Hosts running the PATCHED praisonai >=1.7.2 which still ships a code-mode file (fixed, not vulnerable) — verify package.json/version
  • Source-code mirrors, package caches, or git clones of praisonai-ts that are not actually executed
  • Security researchers' local checkout of the advisory PoC
  • Backup/restore images surfacing historical node_modules
  • Container image-build layers scanned by the EDR during CI
Response runbook
1) Triage: for each hit, resolve the installed praisonai version (read package.json near the FolderPath or query the package manager) and whether codeMode is enabled for any agent. 2) Contain: on confirmed vulnerable (1.4.0–1.7.1) production hosts, disable the codeMode tool and/or upgrade to >=1.7.2; restrict the node process's egress and secrets exposure in the interim. 3) Hunt: correlate the host against UC1 (node spawning shells) and UC3 (node novel egress) to determine whether the vuln was merely present or actually exploited.

PraisonAI codeMode escape: Node.js process spawning OS shell for recon/secret access AIExploitation Medium
Hunts the post-escape behaviour of the PraisonAI codeMode sandbox break: a node host process spawning an OS shell (sh/bash/cmd/powershell) whose command line performs environment/credential/file recon. After `({}).constructor.constructor('return process')()` recovers `process.mainModule.require`, attacker code calls child_process to run commands the sandbox was meant to block. A node process that is only supposed to run 'sandboxed' JS should never shell out to read /etc/passwd, .env or printenv. Rationale: Uses the article's stated post-escape impact (child_process subprocess execution, reading env vars and host files) anchored on a node parent process — the one binary that hosts codeMode. Recon-token filter on the child command line keeps it from firing on every legitimate node->shell call. Cross-checked GHSA and the GitLab advisory mirror (fixed 1.7.2) plus public vm-escape write-ups showing the canonical execSync('cat /etc/passwd') payload. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/praisonai/GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp/ • https://thegoodhacker.com/posts/the-unsecure-node-vm-module/ • https://github.com/vercel/workflow/issues/1026
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1059.003 T1190
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Node app legitimately shelling out via child_process to git/imagemagick/ffmpeg during normal operation
  • npm/yarn lifecycle scripts (postinstall) invoking bash that runs uname/base64 during builds
  • node-gyp compiling native modules and reading system files
  • CI/CD runners using a node orchestrator to launch bash provisioning steps
  • Monitoring/backup agents written in Node that call whoami/hostname for inventory
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("node-gyp","npm ","yarn ","prebuild","node_modules/.bin","/usr/lib/node_modules") or ProcessCommandLine has_any ("node-gyp","prebuild-install"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full child command line and the node parent's command line/working dir; confirm whether this host runs a praisonai/codeMode agent endpoint and whether the recon target (.env, /etc/passwd, printenv) is legitimate for that service. 2) Contain: if unexplained, isolate the host, rotate any API keys/service tokens reachable from the node process environment, and quarantine the node service pending review. 3) Hunt: search the same host for sibling node->shell executions, outbound connections from node to new domains/IPs (exfil), and any process command line containing 'constructor.constructor' or 'process.mainModule.require'; pin the praisonai package version and upgrade to >=1.7.2.

PraisonAI codeMode Function-constructor prototype-chain escape payload signature AIExploitation High
Hunts the exact JavaScript escape primitive used to break out of codeMode's fake sandbox: the prototype-chain recovery of the real Function constructor (`({}).constructor.constructor('return process')()`) combined with `process.mainModule.require`. When this payload transits a command line, application/syslog stream, or captured request body it is a near-zero-FP indicator of an attempted codeMode (or any Node vm/new Function) sandbox escape. Rationale: Targets the literal escape gadget the advisory documents (`({}).constructor.constructor('return process')()` -> `process.mainModule.require`), which the blocklist never covers; the two-token pairing (constructor.constructor + mainModule/return process) makes legitimate occurrence vanishingly rare. Datadog uses case-insensitive full-text phrase search so no casing enumeration is needed. Cross-checked against public Node vm/new Function escape research confirming the same gadget. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/praisonai/GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp/ • https://thegoodhacker.com/posts/the-unsecure-node-vm-module/ • https://architecture.pub/articles/410978448.html
ATT&CKT1059.007 T1211
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • SAST/dependency scanners or pentest tooling replaying the advisory PoC against your own apps
  • The PraisonAI regression/PoV test (poc/pov_poc.js) executing in CI or a developer sandbox
  • Security researchers or red team validating GHSA-vmmj-pfw7-fjwp in a lab
  • Application logs verbosely echoing a blocked/sanitised request body that contained the payload
  • Developer documentation or a blog/notebook about Node sandbox escapes being opened/built on the host
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("pov_poc.js","semgrep","trivy","grype","npm test","jest","mocha") or FolderPath has_any ("/poc/","\\test\\","/test/","node_modules"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: capture the full payload and its source (which process/log stream, which app endpoint, which user/IP submitted it) and determine whether it reached a live codeMode/vm sink or was logged after being blocked. 2) Contain: block the source IP/user at the agent/tool API, take the affected node service out of rotation, and rotate any secrets present in that process environment (API keys, service tokens). 3) Hunt: correlate with UC1 (node spawning shells) on the same host and time window, review outbound connections from the node process for exfil, confirm praisonai is patched to >=1.7.2, and audit other tool endpoints that funnel user/LLM content into new Function or vm.

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Unauthenticated POST to PraisonAI /api/mcp/connect MCP endpoint
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] PraisonAI UI (aiui) server spawns shell/LOLBin child via unauth MCP connect RCE · [LLM] PraisonAI MCP RCE proof-of-concept marker file written to /tmp · Article-specific behavioural hunt — [GHSA / CRITICAL] GHSA-p75f-6fp4-p57w: PraisonAI: Missing Authentication for Cri
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
PraisonAI UI (aiui) server spawns shell/LOLBin child via unauth MCP connect RCE AIExploitation High
Hunts the post-exploitation execution step of GHSA-p75f-6fp4-p57w: the PraisonAI UI host app (launched via `aiui run` / `praisonai ui` / `praisonai claw`) starting an attacker-chosen local process through the MCP stdio transport. Legitimate MCP stdio servers are npx/uvx/node/python runtimes; a raw shell, touch, curl, wget or nc child of the aiui server is the RCE signature. Rationale: Pivots on the article's exact server-launch strings (`aiui run`, `praisonai ui`, `praisonai claw` from ui.py/claw.py) as the parent and a raw shell/LOLBin child — no legitimate MCP stdio server is a bare shell. Datadog left empty because linux.auditd EXECVE in this tenant exposes no parent-process field to anchor the aiui pivot. Falcon limited to ParentBaseFileName since FDR ProcessRollup2 carries no parent command line. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-p75f-6fp4-p57w • https://advisories.gitlab.com/pkg/npm/mcp-bridge/GHSA-wvr4-3wq4-gpc5/ • https://www.sysdig.com/blog/cve-2026-44338-praisonai-authentication-bypass-in-under-4-hours-and-the-growing-trend-of-rapid-exploitation • https://github.com/MervinPraison/PraisonAI/security/advisories/GHSA-9qhq-v63v-fv3j
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.004 T1059
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • A legitimately-configured MCP server whose stdio command is a shell wrapper script (e.g. a `bash -c` launcher around a real MCP binary)
  • Admin/SRE debugging the aiui service from the same process tree (interactive shell under the service)
  • An MCP server config that uses curl/wget to fetch from a package registry on startup
  • Health-check or readiness probe scripts shelling out under the service account
Suppression filter
| where not (FileName in~ ("curl","wget") and ProcessCommandLine has_any ("registry.npmjs.org","pypi.org","ghcr.io","objects.githubusercontent.com"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full ChildCmd and ParentCmd — confirm the child is a real shell/exfil tool, not an MCP runtime, and check whether the aiui service is internet-exposed (bound 0.0.0.0). 2) Contain: kill the child PID, isolate/cordon the host, and immediately set `AUTH_ENFORCE=true` (or take the UI offline) so /api/mcp/connect is no longer unauthenticated; rotate any secrets readable by the UI service user. 3) Hunt: review proxy/web logs for prior POSTs to /api/mcp/connect, enumerate sibling children of the aiui process, and check for follow-on persistence (cron, systemd units, SSH keys) written by the same account.

| order by Timestamp desc

Unauthenticated POST to PraisonAI /api/mcp/connect MCP endpoint AIDelivery Medium
Hunts inbound HTTP requests to the vulnerable PraisonAIUI route `POST /api/mcp/connect` (and `/api/mcp/servers`) exposed by the UI host app on 0.0.0.0 without auth. Surfaces exploitation attempts at the wire level before code executes, complementing the host-side process detection. Rationale: Uses the article's exact route strings `/api/mcp/connect` and `/api/mcp/servers` (registered at praisonaiui/server.py:2769-2770) plus the POST method from the documented PoC request. Defender/Falcon/Datadog/CloudWatch empty because the request body and URI live only in proxy/web-access logs, not in EDR or AWS CloudTrail telemetry in this tenant. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-p75f-6fp4-p57w • https://advisories.gitlab.com/pkg/npm/mcp-bridge/GHSA-wvr4-3wq4-gpc5/
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • A legitimate operator adding an MCP server through the PraisonAI UI, which issues the same POST /api/mcp/connect from the browser
  • Internal vulnerability scanner or ASM probe hitting the endpoint
  • CI/CD smoke test that exercises the MCP connect route
  • Load balancer / uptime monitor configured to POST to the API path
Response runbook
1) Triage: identify the SourceIP — is it external/untrusted or an internal admin workstation? Correlate the timestamp against UC1 process-spawn alerts on the destination host. 2) Contain: if the source is untrusted, block it at the WAF/proxy and restrict the aiui listener to localhost or behind authenticated reverse proxy (set AUTH_ENFORCE=true). 3) Hunt: pull all requests from that SourceIP, look for repeated POSTs (PoC retries until the process wins scheduling) and any subsequent host-side child-process execution.

PraisonAI AgentOS unauthenticated POST /api/chat remote agent invocation AIExploitation High
Hunts unauthenticated HTTP requests to the AgentOS FastAPI routes POST /api/chat and GET /api/agents that return 200 with no Authorization header — the exact incomplete-fix surface in CVE-2026-40151. A no-auth 200 on these paths means an external client successfully enumerated or drove the deployed agent (triggering its tools, memory, and cost-bearing model calls). Rationale: Uses the article's exact incomplete-fix routes (POST /api/chat, GET /api/agents) and the documented behaviour that no-auth requests return 200 (vs 401 on the hardened sibling control). HttpRequestHeaderAuthorization empty + 200 is the clean true positive for unauthenticated invocation; corroborated by the GHSA, the CVE-2026-40151 record, and public reports of rapid endpoint scanning. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-892r-p3jq-jp24 • https://radar.offseq.com/threat/cve-2026-40151-cwe-200-exposure-of-sensitive-infor-41548a8f • https://vulnerability.circl.lu/vuln/ghsa-892r-p3jq-jp24 • https://www.sysdig.com/blog/cve-2026-44338-praisonai-authentication-bypass-in-under-4-hours-and-the-growing-trend-of-rapid-exploitation
ATT&CKT1190 T1213
Data sourcesWeb.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Kubernetes liveness/readiness probes GETting /api/agents (UA kube-probe)
  • Cloud load-balancer health checks (ELB-HealthChecker) hitting the route
  • An upstream API gateway that terminated auth at the edge, so the backend log shows no Authorization header
  • CI/CD smoke tests invoking the deployed agent post-deploy
  • Internal developers calling the API directly on a trusted network where auth was intentionally disabled
Suppression filter
| where SrcIpAddr !in ("127.0.0.1","::1") and HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent !has "kube-probe" and HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent !has "ELB-HealthChecker" and HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent !has "GoogleHC"
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull all 200 responses to /api/chat and /api/agents grouped by source IP; confirm whether the caller is external (non-RFC1918, not a known LB/proxy) and whether the Authorization header was absent. Check the AgentOS process owner and what tools/memory the agent wraps. 2) Contain: place the AgentOS listener behind an authenticating reverse proxy or rebind to 127.0.0.1, or block port 8000 at the perimeter immediately; rotate any API keys/secrets the agent had access to. 3) Hunt: review request bodies for /api/chat to identify what messages were sent (data exfiltration, tool abuse), pivot on the source IP across other internet-facing assets, and check for the GET /api/agents enumeration that typically precedes invocation.
ImWebSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where Url has "/api/chat" or Url has "/api/agents"
| where HttpMethod in~ ("POST","GET")
| where isempty(HttpRequestHeaderAuthorization)   // no bearer token presented
| where HttpStatusCode == 200                      // vulnerable AgentOS returns 200, a patched/proxied one returns 401
| project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, Url, HttpMethod, HttpStatusCode, UserAgent=HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent
| sort by TimeGenerated desc
title: PraisonAI AgentOS Unauthenticated /api/chat or /api/agents Access (CVE-2026-40151)

description: Detects HTTP requests to the PraisonAI AgentOS FastAPI routes /api/chat and /api/agents that return 200, indicating unauthenticated agent enumeration or invocation per CVE-2026-40151 / GHSA-892r-p3jq-jp24.

PraisonAI AgentOS process bound to 0.0.0.0:8000 / external inbound connections AIDelivery Medium
Hunts the misconfiguration root cause: a PraisonAI/uvicorn process launched with the default host=0.0.0.0 port=8000 and/or accepting inbound connections from public IPs on 8000. This is the network-exposure precondition that makes the unauthenticated AgentOS routes reachable. Rationale: Anchors on the article's literal config defaults (host="0.0.0.0", port=8000) and the praisonai/AgentOS/AgentApp module names, plus public inbound on 8000. This catches the exposed-listener root cause regardless of whether an exploit request has landed yet — the 0.0.0.0 default is called out as the root cause in the advisory. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-892r-p3jq-jp24 • https://radar.offseq.com/threat/cve-2026-40151-cwe-200-exposure-of-sensitive-infor-41548a8f
ATT&CKT1190 T1133
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Intentional production deployment with AgentOS on 0.0.0.0 but fronted by an authenticating reverse proxy (the advisory's recommended pattern)
  • Containers binding 0.0.0.0 inside an isolated pod network with no real internet exposure
  • Dev/test instances deliberately reachable on a trusted LAN
  • Public inbound connections that are actually from a known ingress controller / WAF source IP, not real external clients
Suppression filter
| where RemoteIPs !has "10." and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "127.0.0.1"
Response runbook
1) Triage: for each hit, determine whether the host is genuinely internet-facing (DeviceInfo IsInternetFacing / public IP) and whether an authenticating proxy sits in front of port 8000. 2) Contain: if exposed without auth, rebind AgentOS to 127.0.0.1 or place it behind a bearer-auth proxy and firewall port 8000; treat as exploitable per CVE-2026-40151 since v4.5.128–v4.6.57 and main are all vulnerable. 3) Hunt: cross-reference with web-log UC1 to see if any unauthenticated /api/chat or /api/agents 200s already occurred against this listener, and inventory other hosts running the same praisonai version.

| order by FirstSeen desc

Vulnerable PraisonAI package inventory (CVE-2026-40151, >= 4.2.1 <= 4.6.57) AIReconnaissance Medium
Surfaces hosts with the praisonai / praisonaiagents package installed in the confirmed-vulnerable range (incl. the supposedly-patched v4.5.128 and latest v4.6.57). Used to scope exposure for the unauthenticated AgentOS incomplete-fix advisory. This is posture scoping: it tells the SOC which assets need the network-exposure and web-log detections applied. Data sources
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment

Response runbook

Unauthenticated PraisonAI AgentTeam.launch agent invocation via /agents endpoints AIExploitation Medium
Hunts external HTTP requests that hit PraisonAI's unauthenticated AgentTeam.launch()/Agents.launch() routes: GET /{path}/list (agent enumeration), POST /{path} (sequential team invocation) and POST /{path}/{agent_id} (single-agent invocation), defaulting to /agents. Because the launch path registers no auth middleware, every 200 response is by definition unauthenticated agent execution (GHSA-x8cv-xmq7-p8xp, sibling of CVE-2026-44338 / CVE-2026-40151). Rationale: Anchored on the article's concrete route shapes (GET /{path}/list, POST /{path}, POST /{path}/{agent_id}; default path /agents from the PoC) plus the fact that no Authorization parsing exists in the launch path, so any 200 from a public source IP is unauthenticated agent execution. Corroborated by sibling advisories and observed in-the-wild scanning of /agents and /chat. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-6rmh-7xcm-cpxj • https://www.sysdig.com/blog/cve-2026-44338-praisonai-authentication-bypass-in-under-4-hours-and-the-growing-trend-of-rapid-exploitation • https://gbhackers.com/praisonai-vulnerability-actively-exploited/ • https://advisories.gitlab.com/pkg/pypi/praisonai/CVE-2026-39889/
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate internal application/front-end calling its own PraisonAI /agents API from a corporate egress IP that resolves as public
  • Authorized API consumers or partner integrations invoking the agent team intentionally
  • Security scanners / ASM tooling (e.g. internal pentest, Nuclei templates) probing /agents/list
  • Uptime monitors polling the documented /health and adjacent routes
  • DAST pipelines in CI hitting the staging endpoint
Suppression filter
| where UserAgents !has_any ("PraisonAI-internal", "uptime-monitor")
Response runbook
1) Triage: Pull the full request set for the offending CIp — confirm whether /agents/list (enumeration) preceded a POST /agents or POST /agents/{id} (invocation), inspect User-Agent and request bodies for prompt-injection or data-exfil markers, and verify the source IP is not a sanctioned consumer. 2) Contain: Put the PraisonAI launch endpoint behind auth or network ACLs immediately (the launch path ships no token check); block the source IP at the WAF/reverse proxy and, if internet-exposed, take the service off 0.0.0.0. 3) Hunt: Review all historical hits on the route shapes, pivot on the host running the server for outbound connections or tool/agent actions triggered by the injected prompts, and confirm whether the deployment is on a patched/hardened build vs. current main.
AppServiceHTTPLogs
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ScStatus == 200
| where (CsMethod == "GET" and tolower(CsUriStem) endswith "/agents/list")
     or (CsMethod == "POST" and tolower(CsUriStem) matches regex @"/agents(/[^/]+)?$")
| where not(ipv4_is_private(CIp))
| summarize Requests = count(), Methods = make_set(CsMethod), Paths = make_set(CsUriStem), UserAgents = make_set(UserAgent, 5), FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated) by CIp, CsHost
| order by Requests desc

Internet-facing Python host running PraisonAI Agents.launch bound to 0.0.0.0 AIDelivery Low
Hunts hosts where a Python/uvicorn process running the PraisonAI agent server accepts inbound connections from public source IPs — the exposure precondition for GHSA-x8cv-xmq7-p8xp, where Agents.launch()/AgentTeam.launch() default-binds to 0.0.0.0 and documentation shows host="0.0.0.0". Surfaces the externally reachable, unauthenticated agent-execution surface before/while it is probed. Rationale: Uses the article's specific exposure facts: launch() default bind host 0.0.0.0 and documented host="0.0.0.0" for remote access, hosted by a Python/uvicorn process, accepting public inbound. Process-name + public-inbound + praisonai cmdline/path keeps it scoped to the vulnerable surface rather than generic Python listeners. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-6rmh-7xcm-cpxj • https://www.sysdig.com/blog/cve-2026-44338-praisonai-authentication-bypass-in-under-4-hours-and-the-growing-trend-of-rapid-exploitation
ATT&CKT1190 T1133
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: high — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Intentionally exposed, but properly proxied, PraisonAI deployment fronted by an auth gateway
  • Dev/test Python services (Flask/uvicorn) unrelated to PraisonAI that match on python.exe
  • Internal load-balancer or health-check traffic that egresses and returns as a public IP
  • Container hosts where many Python microservices accept inbound on a shared node
  • Data-science/Jupyter or API servers legitimately bound to 0.0.0.0 behind a firewall
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("praisonai","AgentTeam")
Response runbook
1) Triage: Confirm the listening Python process is actually PraisonAI (inspect InitiatingProcessCommandLine/FolderPath and the listening LocalPort) and determine whether the public inbound sources are sanctioned consumers or opportunistic scanners. 2) Contain: If it is an unauthenticated PraisonAI launch surface, rebind to 127.0.0.1 or place it behind an authenticating reverse proxy / network ACL, and restrict the security group/firewall to known clients. 3) Hunt: Correlate with web/proxy logs (UC1) for hits on /agents/list and POST /agents from the same source IPs, check the host for prompt-injection-driven tool actions or outbound connections, and inventory other hosts running the same vulnerable build.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where ActionType == "InboundConnectionAccepted"
| where RemoteIPType == "Public"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("python.exe","python3.exe","pythonw.exe","uvicorn.exe")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("praisonai","AgentTeam",".launch","Agents") or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has "praisonai"
| summarize Connections = count(), DistinctSources = dcount(RemoteIP), Ports = make_set(LocalPort), SampleCmd = any(InitiatingProcessCommandLine), FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath
| order by Connections desc
